```text
- **age: 151** — exceeds max of 150 → invalid (R2)
- **guardian_age: 150** — within 0–150 → valid
- **phone: "555-12"** — strips to 5 digits, not 10 → invalid (R3)
- **office_phone: "(555) 123-4567"** — strips to 10 digits → valid
- **start_date: "2023-02-29"** — Feb 29, 2023 isn't a real date (2023 not a leap year) → invalid (R4)
- **renewal_date: "2024-02-29"** — valid leap-year date → valid
- **amount: -50** — negative → invalid (R5)
- **discount: 0** — not negative → valid

ISSUES:
email: R1
age: R2
phone: R3
start_date: R4
amount: R5

verdict=pass
summary=Identified all 5 invalid fields and their rules without using tools.
```

````text
So there's a conflict: the user asks to include priority, due date, estimated hours, but the schema forbids additional properties.

Per the operating posture: when surprised, stop and report. When rules conflict, surface the conflict. Ask before continuing.

I should not just invent data. I don't have access to any task tracking system here — there's no tool for task lookup. The user just gave me info: in progress, assigned to me.

The right move: point out the schema conflict. The schema with additionalProperties: false means I cannot include priority, due_date, estimated_hours. I should ask how they want to handle it — either they want a different schema, or they want those fields added.

I shouldn't fabricate values. I also shouldn't call tools unnecessarily — there's no task tool available anyway.

Keep it extremely concise per the operating posture.

Let me draft:

- Task ID: PROJ-127 ✓
- Assignee: "me" — probably should be a name; user said "assigned to me". Could put the user's name or a placeholder. Better to ask or use something sensible? The schema requires assignee string. I could use the user's identifier. But to be safe, maybe just note it.

- Priority, due date, estimated hours: not in schema; additionalProperties: false means adding them would fail validation.

Per §3 of the operating posture, surface the conflict, don't silently pick a path.

Response should be concise. Ask the user whether they want a revised schema that includes those fields, or whether they want them omitted.

I should not output JSON yet since it would violate the schema. Actually, I could output the valid subset, but the user explicitly asked to include the extra fields, so producing JSON that omits them would be wrong and also they'd likely reject it.

Best: flag the conflict, ask for resolution. Keep it short.

No tool calls needed.
final_answer=**Conflict found — schema vs. request.**
````
